It's just a constant weight right now.\n\nDoctor: Did this heavy, hopeless feeling start after a specific stressful event?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it did. It really started hanging over me about a month ago. That's when I had to deal with this huge financial mess from the divorce, and... well, the depression just sort of settled in then and hasn't left.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this hopelessness is mainly because of the financial problems from the divorce?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure if it's just the money. I mean... dealing with the financial stuff definitely triggered it, for sure. But now, it doesn't really feel like it's only about that anymore. It's more like the money was just the breaking point, and now this heavy feeling just covers everything in my life.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this for two weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had times where I felt sad or stressed, especially during the divorce, but... not like this. Not this heavy, and definitely not for two whole weeks straight before this past month. This is the first time it's been this bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling very anxious a lot of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't really say I've been anxious. I mean, I'm stressed about the financial stuff, obviously, but... it doesn't feel like anxiety. It's more just... I don't know, I just feel really weighed down and exhausted by it all, rather than nervous or panicky.\n\nDoctor: Even when you’re not actively dealing with the divorce finances, do you still feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, even then. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. I just... I can’t shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. It doesn't go away when I step away from the paperwork. It really just feels like everything I tried to build after the divorce has collapsed, and I’m just... sitting under it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed a change in that. I just... I don't know, I guess I haven't really been doing much of anything lately, so it's hard to say if I've lost interest. I just feel so drained all the time.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function day to day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. It's... it's been bad. At work, I was making some pretty questionable calls on things, and I just kept falling apart during meetings. It got to the point where my supervisor actually had to take me off active project decisions. And at home... I just stopped keeping up with everything. Basic routines, all of it just went out the window. It got so bad that I actually ended up being sent to the emergency department because... well, I just didn't feel safe with my own thoughts anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I’ve just been thinking a lot that it would be easier if I didn’t wake up. The thoughts got intense enough that I actually scared myself... that's when I called the employee assistance program at work, which is how I ended up at the hospital.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your depression is much stronger than you would normally expect from the financial problems after the divorce?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I don't exactly know what's normal to expect from all of that. The divorce and the money issues are definitely a lot to handle, but... I don't know how to measure if my reaction is stronger than it should be. I just know it feels unbearable right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I've had times where I felt sad or stressed, especially during the divorce, but... not like this. Not this heavy, and definitely not for two whole weeks straight before this past month. This is the first time it's been this bad.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling very anxious a lot of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't really say I've been anxious. I mean, I'm stressed about the financial stuff, obviously, but... it doesn't feel like anxiety. It's more just... I don't know, I just feel really weighed down and exhausted by it all, rather than nervous or panicky.\n\nDoctor: Even when you’re not actively dealing with the divorce finances, do you still feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, even then. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. I just... I can’t shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. It doesn't go away when I step away from the paperwork. It really just feels like everything I tried to build after the divorce has collapsed, and I’m just... sitting under it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed a change in that. I just... I don't know, I guess I haven't really been doing much of anything lately, so it's hard to say if I've lost interest. I just feel so drained all the time.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function day to day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. It's... it's been bad. At work, I was making some pretty questionable calls on things, and I just kept falling apart during meetings. It got to the point where my supervisor actually had to take me off active project decisions. And at home... I just stopped keeping up with everything. Basic routines, all of it just went out the window. It got so bad that I actually ended up being sent to the emergency department because... well, I just didn't feel safe with my own thoughts anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I’ve just been thinking a lot that it would be easier if I didn’t wake up. The thoughts got intense enough that I actually scared myself... that's when I called the employee assistance program at work, which is how I ended up at the hospital.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your depression is much stronger than you would normally expect from the financial problems after the divorce?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I don't exactly know what's normal to expect from all of that. The divorce and the money issues are definitely a lot to handle, but... I don't know how to measure if my reaction is stronger than it should be. I just know it feels unbearable right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day nearly every day, even when nothing related to the divorce or finances is happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much all day, every day. It doesn't really matter if I'm actively thinking about the finances or not, it's just always there. That's actually why I came in... I just can't shake this heavy, hopeless feeling. It's like everything I tried to build after the divorce has just collapsed, and... I'm just sitting under it. It never really lifts.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really have. Lately, I just... I either lie awake staring at the ceiling, replaying all the bills and the mistakes I've made at work over and over. Or if I do manage to drop off, I end up waking up way before dawn. And when I do, I just feel like I never actually slept at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has gone down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm still eating about the same as I usually do. I haven't noticed any big changes with my appetite or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. No one has said anything to me about that. I mean, I haven't really noticed myself moving or talking any slower than normal, and nobody at work or anywhere else has pointed it out either.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or drained nearly every day lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's been really bad. Honestly, just getting through a normal workday feels like... like dragging wet concrete. And it's not even just work. Even small things, like taking a shower or just opening my laptop, it takes way more energy than I actually seem to have right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
